/Users/hishamel-husseini/Documents/projects/raffle-fun-simplified-settlement/packages/contracts/node_modules/@pythnetwork/entropy-sdk-solidity/EntropyStructs.sol
  1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache 2
  2 |     |
  3 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.0;
  4 |     |
  5 |     | // This contract holds old versions of the Entropy structs that are no longer used for contract storage.
  6 |     | // However, they are still used in EntropyEvents to maintain the public interface of prior versions of
  7 |     | // the Entropy contract.
  8 |     | //
  9 |     | // See EntropyStructsV2 for the struct definitions currently in use.
 10 |     | contract EntropyStructs {
 11 |     |     struct ProviderInfo {
 12 |     |         uint128 feeInWei;
 13 |     |         uint128 accruedFeesInWei;
 14 |     |         // The commitment that the provider posted to the blockchain, and the sequence number
 15 |     |         // where they committed to this. This value is not advanced after the provider commits,
 16 |     |         // and instead is stored to help providers track where they are in the hash chain.
 17 |     |         bytes32 originalCommitment;
 18 |     |         uint64 originalCommitmentSequenceNumber;
 19 |     |         // Metadata for the current commitment. Providers may optionally use this field to help
 20 |     |         // manage rotations (i.e., to pick the sequence number from the correct hash chain).
 21 |     |         bytes commitmentMetadata;
 22 |     |         // Optional URI where clients can retrieve revelations for the provider.
 23 |     |         // Client SDKs can use this field to automatically determine how to retrieve random values for each provider.
 24 |     |         // TODO: specify the API that must be implemented at this URI
 25 |     |         bytes uri;
 26 |     |         // The first sequence number that is *not* included in the current commitment (i.e., an exclusive end index).
 27 |     |         // The contract maintains the invariant that sequenceNumber <= endSequenceNumber.
 28 |     |         // If sequenceNumber == endSequenceNumber, the provider must rotate their commitment to add additional random values.
 29 |     |         uint64 endSequenceNumber;
 30 |     |         // The sequence number that will be assigned to the next inbound user request.
 31 |     |         uint64 sequenceNumber;
 32 |     |         // The current commitment represents an index/value in the provider's hash chain.
 33 |     |         // These values are used to verify requests for future sequence numbers. Note that
 34 |     |         // currentCommitmentSequenceNumber < sequenceNumber.
 35 |     |         //
 36 |     |         // The currentCommitment advances forward through the provider's hash chain as values
 37 |     |         // are revealed on-chain.
 38 |     |         bytes32 currentCommitment;
 39 |     |         uint64 currentCommitmentSequenceNumber;
 40 |     |         // An address that is authorized to set / withdraw fees on behalf of this provider.
 41 |     |         address feeManager;
 42 |     |         // Maximum number of hashes to record in a request. This should be set according to the maximum gas limit
 43 |     |         // the provider supports for callbacks.
 44 |     |         uint32 maxNumHashes;
 45 |     |     }
 46 |     |
 47 |     |     struct Request {
 48 |     |         // Storage slot 1 //
 49 |     |         address provider;
 50 |     |         uint64 sequenceNumber;
 51 |     |         // The number of hashes required to verify the provider revelation.
 52 |     |         uint32 numHashes;
 53 |     |         // Storage slot 2 //
 54 |     |         // The commitment is keccak256(userCommitment, providerCommitment). Storing the hash instead of both saves 20k gas by
 55 |     |         // eliminating 1 store.
 56 |     |         bytes32 commitment;
 57 |     |         // Storage slot 3 //
 58 |     |         // The number of the block where this request was created.
 59 |     |         // Note that we're using a uint64 such that we have an additional space for an address and other fields in
 60 |     |         // this storage slot. Although block.number returns a uint256, 64 bits should be plenty to index all of the
 61 |     |         // blocks ever generated.
 62 |     |         uint64 blockNumber;
 63 |     |         // The address that requested this random number.
 64 |     |         address requester;
 65 |     |         // If true, incorporate the blockhash of blockNumber into the generated random value.
 66 |     |         bool useBlockhash;
 67 |     |         // True if this is a request that expects a callback.
 68 |     |         bool isRequestWithCallback;
 69 |     |     }
 70 |     | }
 71 |     |

  8 |     |         uint128 accruedFeesInWei;

 10 |     |         // where they committed to this. This value is not advanced after the provider commits,
 11 |     |         // and instead is stored to help providers track where they are in the hash chain.
 12 |     |         bytes32 originalCommitment;
 13 |     |         uint64 originalCommitmentSequenceNumber;
 14 |     |         // Metadata for the current commitment. Providers may optionally use this field to help
 15 |     |         // manage rotations (i.e., to pick the sequence number from the correct hash chain).
 16 |     |         bytes commitmentMetadata;
 17 |     |         // Optional URI where clients can retrieve revelations for the provider.
 18 |     |         // Client SDKs can use this field to automatically determine how to retrieve random values for each provider.
 19 |     |         // TODO: specify the API that must be implemented at this URI
 20 |     |         bytes uri;
 21 |     |         // The first sequence number that is *not* included in the current commitment (i.e., an exclusive end index).
 22 |     |         // The contract maintains the invariant that sequenceNumber <= endSequenceNumber.
 23 |     |         // If sequenceNumber == endSequenceNumber, the provider must rotate their commitment to add additional random values.
 24 |     |         uint64 endSequenceNumber;
 25 |     |         // The sequence number that will be assigned to the next inbound user request.
 26 |     |         uint64 sequenceNumber;
 27 |     |         // The current commitment represents an index/value in the provider's hash chain.
 28 |     |         // These values are used to verify requests for future sequence numbers. Note that
 29 |     |         // currentCommitmentSequenceNumber < sequenceNumber.
 30 |     |         //
 31 |     |         // The currentCommitment advances forward through the provider's hash chain as values
 32 |     |         // are revealed on-chain.
 33 |     |         bytes32 currentCommitment;
 34 |     |         uint64 currentCommitmentSequenceNumber;
 35 |     |         // An address that is authorized to set / withdraw fees on behalf of this provider.
 36 |     |         address feeManager;
 37 |     |         // Maximum number of hashes to record in a request. This should be set according to the maximum gas limit
 38 |     |         // the provider supports for callbacks.
 39 |     |         uint32 maxNumHashes;
 40 |     |         // Default gas limit to use for callbacks.
 41 |     |         uint32 defaultGasLimit;
 42 |     |     }
 43 |     |
 44 |     |     struct Request {
 45 |     |         // Storage slot 1 //
 46 |     |         address provider;
 47 |     |         uint64 sequenceNumber;
 48 |     |         // The number of hashes required to verify the provider revelation.
 49 |     |         uint32 numHashes;
 50 |     |         // Storage slot 2 //
 51 |     |         // The commitment is keccak256(userCommitment, providerCommitment). Storing the hash instead of both saves 20k gas by
 52 |     |         // eliminating 1 store.
 53 |     |         bytes32 commitment;
 54 |     |         // Storage slot 3 //
 55 |     |         // The number of the block where this request was created.
 56 |     |         // Note that we're using a uint64 such that we have an additional space for an address and other fields in
 57 |     |         // this storage slot. Although block.number returns a uint256, 64 bits should be plenty to index all of the
 58 |     |         // blocks ever generated.
 59 |     |         uint64 blockNumber;
 60 |     |         // The address that requested this random number.
 61 |     |         address requester;
 62 |     |         // If true, incorporate the blockhash of blockNumber into the generated random value.
 63 |     |         bool useBlockhash;
 64 |     |         // Status flag for requests with callbacks. See EntropyConstants for the possible values of this flag.
 65 |     |         uint8 callbackStatus;
 66 |     |         // The gasLimit in units of 10k gas. (i.e., 2 = 20k gas). We're using units of 10k in order to fit this
 67 |     |         // field into the remaining 2 bytes of this storage slot. The dynamic range here is 10k - 655M, which should
 68 |     |         // cover all real-world use cases.
 69 |     |         uint16 gasLimit10k;
 70 |     |     }

/Users/hishamel-husseini/Documents/projects/raffle-fun-simplified-settlement/packages/contracts/node_modules/@pythnetwork/entropy-sdk-solidity/IEntropyConsumer.sol
  1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache 2
  2 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.0;
  3 |     |
  4 |     | abstract contract IEntropyConsumer {
  5 |     |     // This method is called by Entropy to provide the random number to the consumer.
  6 |     |     // It asserts that the msg.sender is the Entropy contract. It is not meant to be
  7 |     |     // override by the consumer.
  8 | *   |     function _entropyCallback(
  9 |     |         uint64 sequence,
 10 |     |         address provider,
 11 |     |         bytes32 randomNumber
 12 | *   |     ) external {
 13 |     |         address entropy = getEntropy();
 14 | *   |         require(entropy != address(0), "Entropy address not set");
 15 | *   |         require(msg.sender == entropy, "Only Entropy can call this function");
 16 |     |
 17 | *   |         entropyCallback(sequence, provider, randomNumber);
 18 |     |     }
 19 |     |
 20 |     |     // getEntropy returns Entropy contract address. The method is being used to check that the
 21 |     |     // callback is indeed from Entropy contract. The consumer is expected to implement this method.
 22 |     |     // Entropy address can be found here - https://docs.pyth.network/entropy/contract-addresses
 23 |     |     function getEntropy() internal view virtual returns (address);
 24 |     |
 25 |     |     // This method is expected to be implemented by the consumer to handle the random number.
 26 |     |     // It will be called by _entropyCallback after _entropyCallback ensures that the call is
 27 |     |     // indeed from Entropy contract.
 28 |     |     function entropyCallback(
 29 |     |         uint64 sequence,
 30 |     |         address provider,
 31 |     |         bytes32 randomNumber
 32 |     |     ) internal virtual;
 33 |     | }
 34 |     |

/Users/hishamel-husseini/Documents/projects/raffle-fun-simplified-settlement/packages/contracts/node_modules/@pythnetwork/entropy-sdk-solidity/IEntropyV2.sol
   1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache 2
   2 |     | pragma solidity ^0.8.0;
   3 |     |
   4 |     | import "./EntropyEvents.sol";
   5 |     | import "./EntropyEventsV2.sol";
   6 |     | import "./EntropyStructsV2.sol";
   7 |     |
   8 |     | interface IEntropyV2 is EntropyEventsV2 {
   9 |     |     /// @notice Request a random number using the default provider with default gas limit
  10 |     |     /// @return assignedSequenceNumber A unique identifier for this request
  11 |     |     /// @dev The address calling this function should be a contract that inherits from the IEntropyConsumer interface.
  12 |     |     /// The `entropyCallback` method on that interface will receive a callback with the returned sequence number and
  13 |     |     /// the generated random number.
  14 |     |     ///
  15 |     |     /// `entropyCallback` will be run with the provider's configured default gas limit.
  16 |     |     ///
  17 |     |     /// This method will revert unless the caller provides a sufficient fee (at least `getFeeV2()`) as msg.value.
  18 |     |     /// Note that the fee can change over time. Callers of this method should explicitly compute `getFeeV2()`
  19 |     |     /// prior to each invocation (as opposed to hardcoding a value). Further note that excess value is *not* refunded to the caller.
  20 |     |     ///
  21 |     |     /// Note that this method uses an in-contract PRNG to generate the user's contribution to the random number.
  22 |     |     /// This approach modifies the security guarantees such that a dishonest validator and provider can
  23 |     |     /// collude to manipulate the result (as opposed to a malicious user and provider). That is, the user
  24 |     |     /// now trusts the validator honestly draw a random number. If you wish to avoid this trust assumption,
  25 |     |     /// call a variant of `requestV2` that accepts a `userRandomNumber` parameter.
  26 |     |     function requestV2()
  27 |     |         external
  28 |     |         payable
  29 |     |         returns (uint64 assignedSequenceNumber);
  30 |     |
  31 |     |     /// @notice Request a random number using the default provider with specified gas limit
  32 |     |     /// @param gasLimit The gas limit for the callback function.
  33 |     |     /// @return assignedSequenceNumber A unique identifier for this request
  34 |     |     /// @dev The address calling this function should be a contract that inherits from the IEntropyConsumer interface.
  35 |     |     /// The `entropyCallback` method on that interface will receive a callback with the returned sequence number and
  36 |     |     /// the generated random number.
  37 |     |     ///
  38 |     |     /// `entropyCallback` will be run with the `gasLimit` provided to this function.
  39 |     |     /// The `gasLimit` will be rounded up to a multiple of 10k (e.g., 19000 -> 20000), and furthermore is lower bounded
  40 |     |     /// by the provider's configured default limit.
  41 |     |     ///
  42 |     |     /// This method will revert unless the caller provides a sufficient fee (at least `getFeeV2(gasLimit)`) as msg.value.
  43 |     |     /// Note that the fee can change over time. Callers of this method should explicitly compute `getFeeV2(gasLimit)`
  44 |     |     /// prior to each invocation (as opposed to hardcoding a value). Further note that excess value is *not* refunded to the caller.
  45 |     |     ///
  46 |     |     /// Note that this method uses an in-contract PRNG to generate the user's contribution to the random number.
  47 |     |     /// This approach modifies the security guarantees such that a dishonest validator and provider can
  48 |     |     /// collude to manipulate the result (as opposed to a malicious user and provider). That is, the user
  49 |     |     /// now trusts the validator honestly draw a random number. If you wish to avoid this trust assumption,
  50 |     |     /// call a variant of `requestV2` that accepts a `userRandomNumber` parameter.
  51 |     |     function requestV2(
  52 |     |         uint32 gasLimit
  53 |     |     ) external payable returns (uint64 assignedSequenceNumber);
  54 |     |
  55 |     |     /// @notice Request a random number from a specific provider with specified gas limit
  56 |     |     /// @param provider The address of the provider to request from
  57 |     |     /// @param gasLimit The gas limit for the callback function
  58 |     |     /// @return assignedSequenceNumber A unique identifier for this request
  59 |     |     /// @dev The address calling this function should be a contract that inherits from the IEntropyConsumer interface.
  60 |     |     /// The `entropyCallback` method on that interface will receive a callback with the returned sequence number and
  61 |     |     /// the generated random number.
  62 |     |     ///
  63 |     |     /// `entropyCallback` will be run with the `gasLimit` provided to this function.
  64 |     |     /// The `gasLimit` will be rounded up to a multiple of 10k (e.g., 19000 -> 20000), and furthermore is lower bounded
  65 |     |     /// by the provider's configured default limit.
  66 |     |     ///
  67 |     |     /// This method will revert unless the caller provides a sufficient fee (at least `getFeeV2(provider, gasLimit)`) as msg.value.
  68 |     |     /// Note that provider fees can change over time. Callers of this method should explicitly compute `getFeeV2(provider, gasLimit)`
  69 |     |     /// prior to each invocation (as opposed to hardcoding a value). Further note that excess value is *not* refunded to the caller.
  70 |     |     ///
  71 |     |     /// Note that this method uses an in-contract PRNG to generate the user's contribution to the random number.
  72 |     |     /// This approach modifies the security guarantees such that a dishonest validator and provider can
  73 |     |     /// collude to manipulate the result (as opposed to a malicious user and provider). That is, the user
  74 |     |     /// now trusts the validator honestly draw a random number. If you wish to avoid this trust assumption,
  75 |     |     /// call a variant of `requestV2` that accepts a `userRandomNumber` parameter.
  76 |     |     function requestV2(
  77 |     |         address provider,
  78 |     |         uint32 gasLimit
  79 |     |     ) external payable returns (uint64 assignedSequenceNumber);
  80 |     |
  81 |     |     /// @notice Request a random number from a specific provider with a user-provided random number and gas limit
  82 |     |     /// @param provider The address of the provider to request from
  83 |     |     /// @param userRandomNumber A random number provided by the user for additional entropy
  84 |     |     /// @param gasLimit The gas limit for the callback function. Pass 0 to get a sane default value -- see note below.
  85 |     |     /// @return assignedSequenceNumber A unique identifier for this request
  86 |     |     /// @dev The address calling this function should be a contract that inherits from the IEntropyConsumer interface.
  87 |     |     /// The `entropyCallback` method on that interface will receive a callback with the returned sequence number and
  88 |     |     /// the generated random number.
  89 |     |     ///
  90 |     |     /// `entropyCallback` will be run with the `gasLimit` provided to this function.
  91 |     |     /// The `gasLimit` will be rounded up to a multiple of 10k (e.g., 19000 -> 20000), and furthermore is lower bounded
  92 |     |     /// by the provider's configured default limit.
  93 |     |     ///
  94 |     |     /// This method will revert unless the caller provides a sufficient fee (at least `getFeeV2(provider, gasLimit)`) as msg.value.
  95 |     |     /// Note that provider fees can change over time. Callers of this method should explicitly compute `getFeeV2(provider, gasLimit)`
  96 |     |     /// prior to each invocation (as opposed to hardcoding a value). Further note that excess value is *not* refunded to the caller.
  97 |     |     function requestV2(
  98 |     |         address provider,
  99 |     |         bytes32 userRandomNumber,
 100 |     |         uint32 gasLimit
 101 |     |     ) external payable returns (uint64 assignedSequenceNumber);
 102 |     |
 103 |     |     /// @notice Get information about a specific entropy provider
 104 |     |     /// @param provider The address of the provider to query
 105 |     |     /// @return info The provider information including configuration, fees, and operational status
 106 |     |     /// @dev This method returns detailed information about a provider's configuration and capabilities.
 107 |     |     /// The returned ProviderInfo struct contains information such as the provider's fee structure and gas limits.
 108 |     |     function getProviderInfoV2(
 109 |     |         address provider
 110 |     |     ) external view returns (EntropyStructsV2.ProviderInfo memory info);
 111 |     |
 112 |     |     /// @notice Get the address of the default entropy provider
 113 |     |     /// @return provider The address of the default provider
 114 |     |     /// @dev This method returns the address of the provider that will be used when no specific provider is specified
 115 |     |     /// in the requestV2 calls. The default provider can be used to get the base fee and gas limit information.
 116 |     |     function getDefaultProvider() external view returns (address provider);
 117 |     |
 118 |     |     /// @notice Get information about a specific request
 119 |     |     /// @param provider The address of the provider that handled the request
 120 |     |     /// @param sequenceNumber The unique identifier of the request
 121 |     |     /// @return req The request information including status, random number, and other metadata
 122 |     |     /// @dev This method allows querying the state of a previously made request. The returned Request struct
 123 |     |     /// contains information about whether the request was fulfilled, the generated random number (if available),
 124 |     |     /// and other metadata about the request.
 125 |     |     function getRequestV2(
 126 |     |         address provider,
 127 |     |         uint64 sequenceNumber

 133 |     |     /// the default gas limit. This fee should be passed as msg.value when calling requestV2().

  18 |     | /**
  19 |     |  * @title raffle.fun Raffle Escrow and Bearer Ticket
  20 |     |  * @author Heesho
  21 |     |  * @notice Escrows one NFT, sells transferable tickets, and settles through Pyth Entropy v2 or exact ticket refunds.
  22 |     |  * @dev Each raffle is a non-upgradeable constructor deployment. Ticket ownership is the claim credential: the winning
  23 |     |  *      ticket burns for the NFT or cash award, and refundable tickets burn for their purchase price. No ownership
  24 |     |  *      snapshot or transfer freeze exists. Normal sponsor and treasury proceeds remain pull claims so one recipient
  25 |     |  *      cannot block another. The Entropy callback performs bounded storage work and no external asset transfer.
  26 |     |  * @custom:version 2.0.0
  27 |     |  */

 104 | *   |     function buyTickets(address recipient, uint256 quantity)
 105 |     |         external
 106 |     |         override
 107 |     |         nonReentrant
 108 | *   |         returns (uint256 firstTicketId, uint256 lastTicketId)
 109 | *   |     {
 110 | *   |         _requireStatus(Status.Active);
 111 | *   |         if (block.timestamp < startTime) revert SaleNotStarted(startTime, block.timestamp);
 112 | *   |         if (block.timestamp >= endTime) revert SaleEnded(endTime, block.timestamp);
 113 | *   |         if (recipient == address(0)) revert InvalidRecipient();
 114 | *   |         if (quantity == 0 || quantity > RaffleConstants.MAX_TICKETS_PER_PURCHASE) {
 115 |     |             revert InvalidQuantity(quantity, RaffleConstants.MAX_TICKETS_PER_PURCHASE);
 116 |     |         }
 117 | *   |         if (ticketPrice > type(uint256).max / quantity) revert GrossAmountOverflow();
 118 |     |
 119 | *   |         uint256 grossAmount = ticketPrice * quantity;
 120 | *   |         uint256 balanceBefore = quoteToken.balanceOf(address(this));
 121 | *   |         quoteToken.safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), grossAmount);
 122 | *   |         uint256 balanceAfter = quoteToken.balanceOf(address(this));
 123 | *   |         uint256 receivedAmount = balanceAfter >= balanceBefore ? balanceAfter - balanceBefore : 0;
 124 | *   |         if (receivedAmount != grossAmount) revert UnsupportedQuoteToken(grossAmount, receivedAmount);
 125 |     |
 126 | *   |         grossSales += grossAmount;
 127 | *   |         unsettledPot += grossAmount;

 129 | *   |         lastTicketId = totalTickets + quantity;
 130 | *   |         totalTickets = lastTicketId;
 131 |     |

 133 | *   |             _safeMint(recipient, firstTicketId + offset);
 134 |     |         }
 135 |     |
 136 | *   |         emit TicketsPurchased(msg.sender, recipient, quantity, firstTicketId, lastTicketId, grossAmount);
 137 |     |     }

 418 |     |     /// @dev Clears a normal pull liability before making its exact-transfer ERC-20 interaction.

 420 | *   |         if (to == address(0)) revert ZeroAddress();
 421 | *   |         amount = claimableQuote[account];
 422 | *   |         if (amount == 0) revert NoQuoteClaim(account);
 423 |     |
 424 | *   |         claimableQuote[account] = 0;
 425 | *   |         totalClaimableQuote -= amount;
 426 | *   |         _transferQuoteExact(to, amount);
 427 | *   |         emit QuoteClaimed(account, to, amount);
 428 |     |     }
 429 |     |
 430 |     |     /**
 431 |     |      * @dev Requires the raffle debit and recipient credit to both equal the liability. Reverting restores all effects,
 432 |     |      *      including ticket burns and cleared claims, for fee-on-transfer or sender-tax behavior.
 433 |     |      */
 434 | *   |     function _transferQuoteExact(address to, uint256 amount) internal {
 435 | *   |         if (to == address(this)) revert InvalidQuoteDestination(to);
 436 | *   |         uint256 raffleBalanceBefore = quoteToken.balanceOf(address(this));
 437 | *   |         uint256 recipientBalanceBefore = quoteToken.balanceOf(to);
 438 | *   |         quoteToken.safeTransfer(to, amount);
 439 | *   |         uint256 raffleBalanceAfter = quoteToken.balanceOf(address(this));
 440 | *   |         uint256 recipientBalanceAfter = quoteToken.balanceOf(to);
 441 |     |
 442 | *   |         uint256 debitedAmount = 0;
 443 | *   |         if (raffleBalanceBefore >= raffleBalanceAfter) {
 444 | *   |             debitedAmount = raffleBalanceBefore - raffleBalanceAfter;
 445 |     |         }
 446 | *   |         uint256 creditedAmount =
 447 | *   |             recipientBalanceAfter >= recipientBalanceBefore ? recipientBalanceAfter - recipientBalanceBefore : 0;
 448 | *   |         if (debitedAmount != amount || creditedAmount != amount) {
 449 |     |             revert UnsupportedQuoteTokenTransfer(amount, debitedAmount, creditedAmount);
 450 |     |         }
 451 |     |     }
 452 |     |
 453 |     |     /// @dev Enforces the single authoritative lifecycle value.
 454 | *   |     function _requireStatus(Status expected) internal view {
 455 | *   |         if (status != expected) revert InvalidStatus(status);
 456 |     |     }
 457 |     |
 458 |     |     /// @dev Constructor-time defense against a new raffle owning either of its own fixed claims.
 459 | *   |     function _isConstructorProtocolDestination(address destination, RaffleParams memory params)
 460 |     |         private
 461 |     |         view
 462 | *   |         returns (bool unsafeDestination)
 463 |     |     {
 464 | *   |         unsafeDestination = destination == address(this) || destination == params.factory
 465 | *   |             || destination == params.quoteToken || destination == params.entropy || destination == params.prizeToken;
 466 | *   |         if (!unsafeDestination && destination.code.length != 0) {
 467 |     |             unsafeDestination = IRaffleFactory(params.factory).isRaffle(destination);
 468 |     |         }
 469 |     |     }
 470 |     |
 471 |     |     /// @dev Covers this raffle, its fixed dependencies, its factory, and already registered sibling raffles.
 472 |     |     function _isKnownProtocolDestination(address destination) private view returns (bool unsafeDestination) {
 473 |     |         unsafeDestination = destination == address(this) || destination == factory || destination == address(quoteToken)
 474 |     |             || destination == address(entropy) || destination == address(prizeToken);
 475 |     |         if (!unsafeDestination && destination.code.length != 0) {
 476 |     |             unsafeDestination = IRaffleFactory(factory).isRaffle(destination);
 477 |     |         }
 478 |     |     }

   8 |     | /**
   9 |     |  * @title raffle.fun Raffle Escrow and Bearer Ticket Interface
  10 |     |  * @author Heesho
  11 |     |  * @notice Defines a constructor-deployed raffle whose tickets burn to redeem the winning asset or their own refund.
  12 |     |  * @dev The current ticket owner is the claim owner. Tickets never freeze: the winning ticket remains transferable
  13 |     |  *      until it burns for the NFT or cash award. Every refundable ticket remains transferable until it burns for its
  14 |     |  *      purchase-price refund. Supported quote tokens are non-rebasing exact-transfer ERC-20s. Supported prizes are
  15 |     |  *      honest, standards-compliant ERC-721s.
  16 |     |  * @custom:version 2.0.0
  17 |     |  */
  18 |     | interface IRaffle {
  19 |     |     /// @notice The single authoritative lifecycle and economic result.
  20 |     |     enum Status {
  21 |     |         AwaitingPrize,
  22 |     |         Active,
  23 |     |         Drawing,
  24 |     |         NftWon,
  25 |     |         CashWon,
  26 |     |         Refunding,
  27 |     |         Closed
  28 |     |     }
  29 |     |
  30 |     |     /// @notice Claim type a registered raffle may permissionlessly exercise for another registered raffle.

  52 |     |      * @param endTime Exclusive ticket-sale end.
  53 |     |      * @param callbackGasLimit Gas limit supplied to Entropy v2.

  76 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a required account or destination is zero.
  77 |     |     error ZeroAddress();
  78 |     |     /// @notice Raised when an action requires another lifecycle status.
  79 |     |     error InvalidStatus(Status actual);
  80 |     |     /// @notice Raised when the factory-operated safe deposit differs from the configured prize.

   1 |     | // SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
   2 |     | pragma solidity 0.8.36;
   3 |     |
   4 |     | /**
   5 |     |  * @title raffle.fun USDC Raffle Factory Interface
   6 |     |  * @author Heesho
   7 |     |  * @notice Defines constructor deployment and canonical registry reads for raffles using one factory-wide USDC token.
   8 |     |  * @dev The quote token, Entropy deployment, and callback gas limit are immutable. Factory ownership controls only the
   9 |     |  *      treasury captured by future raffles and whether new creation is paused; existing raffles remain autonomous.
  10 |     |  * @custom:version 2.0.0
  11 |     |  */
  12 |     | interface IRaffleFactory {
  13 |     |     /**
  14 |     |      * @notice Parameters defining a new raffle's prize, economics, recovery destination, timing, and metadata.
  15 |     |      * @param prizeToken Standards-compliant ERC-721 collection containing the prize.
  16 |     |      * @param prizeTokenId Token ID deposited atomically as the prize.
  17 |     |      * @param sponsorPrizeRecoveryRecipient Fixed sponsor-side NFT recovery recipient; zero defaults to the sponsor.
  18 |     |      * @param ticketPrice Gross raw USDC units paid per ticket.
  19 |     |      * @param minimumTickets Threshold at which successful randomness awards the NFT rather than cash fallback.
  20 |     |      * @param startTime Inclusive sale start; zero normalizes to the creation timestamp.
  21 |     |      * @param endTime Exclusive sale end, bounded by the factory's maximum duration.
  22 |     |      * @param metadataURI Constrained metadata URI shared by the raffle and ticket NFTs.
  23 |     |      */
  24 |     |     struct CreateRaffleParams {
  25 |     |         address prizeToken;
  26 |     |         uint256 prizeTokenId;
  27 |     |         address sponsorPrizeRecoveryRecipient;
  28 |     |         uint256 ticketPrice;
  29 |     |         uint256 minimumTickets;
  30 |     |         uint256 startTime;
  31 |     |         uint256 endTime;
  32 |     |         string metadataURI;
  33 |     |     }
  34 |     |
  35 |     |     /// @notice Raised when the required owner or treasury is zero.
  36 |     |     error ZeroAddress();
  37 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a long-lived token or Entropy dependency is zero or has no deployed code.
  38 |     |     error NotContract(address account);
  39 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a proposed prize contract does not report ERC-721 support.
  40 |     |     error UnsupportedPrizeToken(address prizeToken);
  41 |     |     /// @notice Raised when creation is disabled for future raffles.
  42 |     |     error CreationPaused();
  43 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a raffle would sell free tickets.
  44 |     |     error ZeroTicketPrice();
  45 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a raffle has no reachable NFT-award threshold.
  46 |     |     error ZeroMinimumTickets();
  47 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a nonzero scheduled start precedes creation.
  48 |     |     error StartTimeInPast(uint256 startTime, uint256 currentTime);
  49 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a scheduled start exceeds the immutable custody bound.
  50 |     |     error StartTimeTooDistant(uint256 startTime, uint256 maximumStartTime);
  51 |     |     /// @notice Raised when the exclusive sale end does not follow the inclusive start.
  52 |     |     error InvalidEndTime(uint256 startTime, uint256 endTime);
  53 |     |     /// @notice Raised when the requested sale duration exceeds the immutable maximum.
  54 |     |     error SaleDurationTooLong(uint256 duration, uint256 maximumDuration);
  55 |     |     /// @notice Raised when metadata would make deployment unreasonably expensive.
  56 |     |     error MetadataURITooLong(uint256 actualLength, uint256 maximumLength);
  57 |     |     /// @notice Raised when Entropy callbacks would be requested with no gas allowance.
  58 |     |     error ZeroCallbackGasLimit();
  59 |     |     /// @notice Raised when a fixed claimant would be a known non-callable protocol address.
  60 |     |     error UnsafeProtocolDestination(address destination);
  61 |     |     /// @notice Raised when the post-deposit ownership or activation check fails.
  62 |     |     error PrizeEscrowVerificationFailed(address raffle, address prizeToken, uint256 prizeTokenId);
  63 |     |
  64 |     |     /**
  65 |     |      * @notice Emitted after one constructor-deployed raffle is registered, funded, and verified atomically.
  66 |     |      */
  67 |     |     event RaffleCreated(
  68 |     |         uint256 indexed raffleId,
  69 |     |         address indexed raffle,
  70 |     |         address indexed sponsor,
  71 |     |         address sponsorPrizeRecoveryRecipient,
  72 |     |         address prizeToken,
  73 |     |         uint256 prizeTokenId,
  74 |     |         address quoteToken,
  75 |     |         address protocolTreasury,
  76 |     |         uint256 ticketPrice,
  77 |     |         uint256 minimumTickets,
  78 |     |         uint256 startTime,
  79 |     |         uint256 endTime,
  80 |     |         uint256 requestGraceDeadline,
  81 |     |         string metadataURI
  82 |     |     );
  83 |     |
  84 |     |     /// @notice Emitted when the treasury captured only by future raffles changes.
  85 |     |     event ProtocolTreasuryUpdated(address indexed previousTreasury, address indexed newTreasury);
  86 |     |     /// @notice Emitted when creation of future raffles is paused or resumed.
  87 |     |     event CreationPauseUpdated(bool previousPaused, bool newPaused);

 32 |     |     /// @notice Callback gas limit registered for each sequence.

 42 | *   |         fee = newFee;
 43 |     |     }

 46 |     |     function setFeeReadReverts(bool reverts_) external {
 47 |     |         feeReadReverts = reverts_;
 48 |     |     }
 49 |     |
 50 |     |     /// @notice Returns the configured fee for any callback limit.
 51 |     |     /// @return feeAmount Current deterministic fee.
 52 | *   |     function getFeeV2(uint32) external view returns (uint128 feeAmount) {
 53 | *   |         if (feeReadReverts) revert();

 55 |     |     }

 57 |     |     /// @notice Registers a deterministic sequence for the calling consumer.
 58 |     |     /// @param callbackGasLimit Requested callback limit.

 60 | *   |     function requestV2(uint32 callbackGasLimit) external payable returns (uint64 assignedSequenceNumber) {
 61 | *   |         if (msg.value < fee) revert InsufficientFee(fee, msg.value);
 62 | *   |         assignedSequenceNumber = ++latestSequenceNumber;

 84 | *   |             callbackSequence, address(this), randomNumber
